- [ ] **Step 8: Implement the settings reader**

Create `embry0/services/pr_review/settings.py`:

```python
"""A repo's pull request review settings, as stored in repo_preferences.pr_review (#30)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

DEPTHS: tuple[str, ...] = ("full", "targeted", "read_only")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ReviewSettings:
    enabled: bool = False
    depth: str = "full"
    model: str | None = None  # None = the deployment's heavy tier


def review_settings(prefs: Mapping[str, Any] | None) -> ReviewSettings:
    """The settings, defaults for anything unset or malformed. Never raises."""
    raw = (prefs or {}).get("pr_review")
    if not isinstance(raw, Mapping):
        return ReviewSettings()
    depth = raw.get("depth")
    model = raw.get("model")
    return ReviewSettings(
        enabled=raw.get("enabled") is True,
        depth=depth if isinstance(depth, str) and depth in DEPTHS else "full",
        model=model.strip() if isinstance(model, str) and model.strip() else None,
    )


def effective_depth(settings: ReviewSettings, *, from_fork: bool) -> str:
    """A fork is always read-only: code a job runs can read the sandbox's credentials (ADR 0008)."""
    return "read_only" if from_fork else settings.depth
```

- [ ] **Step 9: Implement the dispatcher**

Create `embry0/services/pr_review/dispatcher.py`:

```python
"""Turns a review trigger into a pr-review job, or says why not (#30).

Every trigger — a pull request becoming ready, a ``/embry0 review`` comment,
``embry0 review`` on the CLI — ends in :meth:`PrReviewDispatcher._admit`, so
the gates apply in one order everywhere:

1. the repo is registered and its ``pr_review.enabled`` is on (the CLI skips
   the setting, never the registration);
2. the pull request is not embry0's own;
3. a comment's author has write access;
4. the trigger key is new (``pr_reviews.trigger_key`` is UNIQUE);
5. the budget gate allows the actor.

A refusal is logged with its reason and posts nothing.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import uuid
from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

import structlog

from embry0.orchestration import dispatch_actors
from embry0.services.pr_review.settings import ReviewSettings, effective_depth, review_settings
from embry0.services.pr_review.triggers import (
    PullRequestInfo,
    ReviewCommand,
    ReviewTrigger,
    command_from_github,
    command_from_gitlab,
    command_key,
    is_embry0_branch,
    pr_from_github,
    pr_from_gitlab,
    ready_trigger_from_github,
    ready_trigger_from_gitlab,
)
from embry0.trackers.author_association import DEFAULT_AUTHOR_ROLES, check_comment_author, parse_author_roles

logger = structlog.get_logger(__name__)

GITLAB_DEVELOPER_ACCESS = 30
REVIEW_BODY_CHARS = 6000
# Jobs that carry a pull request's URL without having opened it: a follow-up
# on someone's pull request, and the reviews themselves.
_NOT_AUTHORING_PIPELINES = frozenset({"pr-followup", "pr-review"})


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class DispatchResult:
    dispatched: bool
    reason: str
    job_id: str | None = None

    def as_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        out: dict[str, Any] = {"dispatched": self.dispatched, "reason": self.reason}
        if self.job_id:
            out["job_id"] = self.job_id
        return out


def build_review_config(
    trigger: ReviewTrigger, *, repo: str, settings: ReviewSettings, review_id: str
) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """The job's ``pipeline_config``: everything the pipeline needs, frozen at trigger time."""
    pr = trigger.pr
    return {
        "pipeline": "pr-review",
        "review_id": review_id,
        "trigger_key": trigger.trigger_key,
        "repo": repo,
        "forge": pr.forge,
        "number": pr.number,
        "url": pr.url,
        "title": pr.title[:500],
        "body": pr.body[:REVIEW_BODY_CHARS],
        "author": pr.author,
        "head_ref": pr.head_ref,
        "head_sha": pr.head_sha,
        "base_ref": pr.base_ref,
        "from_fork": pr.from_fork,
        "trigger": trigger.trigger,
        "depth": effective_depth(settings, from_fork=pr.from_fork),
        "model": settings.model,
    }


class PrReviewDispatcher:
    """Applies the review gates and asks the executor for the job."""

    def __init__(
        self,
        *,
        config: Any,
        repos_repo: Any,
        prefs_repo: Any,
        jobs_repo: Any,
        reviews_repo: Any,
        executor: Any,
        github: Any = None,
        gitlab: Any = None,
    ) -> None:
        self._config = config
        self._repos = repos_repo
        self._prefs = prefs_repo
        self._jobs = jobs_repo
        self._reviews = reviews_repo
        self._executor = executor
        self._github = github
        self._gitlab = gitlab

    # ---- entry points ------------------------------------------------------

    async def handle_github_pull_request(self, payload: Mapping[str, Any], action: str) -> DispatchResult | None:
        trigger = ready_trigger_from_github(payload, action)
        return None if trigger is None else await self._dispatch_ready(trigger)

    async def handle_gitlab_merge_request(self, payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> DispatchResult | None:
        trigger = ready_trigger_from_gitlab(payload)
        return None if trigger is None else await self._dispatch_ready(trigger)

    async def handle_github_comment(self, payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> DispatchResult | None:
        command = command_from_github(payload)
        return None if command is None else await self._dispatch_command(command)

    async def handle_gitlab_note(self, payload: Mapping[str, Any]) -> DispatchResult | None:
        command = command_from_gitlab(payload)
        return None if command is None else await self._dispatch_command(command)

    async def dispatch_manual(self, *, repo: str, number: int, actor: str) -> DispatchResult:
        """``embry0 review`` / ``POST /jobs {pipeline: pr-review}``: skips the enabled setting only."""
        registered = await self._repos.registered_as(repo)
        if registered is None:
            return self._refuse("repo_not_registered", repo=repo, number=number)
        forge = str(await self._repos.forge_for(registered))
        pr = await self._fetch_pr(forge, registered, number)
        if pr is None:
            return self._refuse("pr_unreadable", repo=registered, number=number)
        if not pr.is_open:
            return self._refuse("pr_not_open", repo=registered, number=number)
        trigger = ReviewTrigger(
            pr=pr,
            trigger="command",
            trigger_key=command_key(forge, f"manual-{uuid.uuid4().hex[:12]}"),
            actor=actor,
        )
        settings = review_settings(await self._prefs.get(registered))
        return await self._admit(trigger, registered, settings, command=None)

    # ---- gates -------------------------------------------------------------

    async def _dispatch_ready(self, trigger: ReviewTrigger) -> DispatchResult:
        enabled = await self._enabled(trigger.pr.repo, number=trigger.pr.number)
        if isinstance(enabled, DispatchResult):
            return enabled
        registered, settings = enabled
        return await self._admit(trigger, registered, settings, command=None)

    async def _dispatch_command(self, command: ReviewCommand) -> DispatchResult:
        enabled = await self._enabled(command.repo, number=command.number)
        if isinstance(enabled, DispatchResult):
            return enabled
        registered, settings = enabled
        if command.forge == "github":
            pr = await self._fetch_pr("github", registered, command.number)
        else:
            pr = pr_from_gitlab(command.gitlab_mr or {}, repo=registered)
        if pr is None:
            return self._refuse("pr_unreadable", repo=registered, number=command.number)
        if not pr.is_open:
            return self._refuse("pr_not_open", repo=registered, number=command.number)
        trigger = ReviewTrigger(
            pr=pr,
            trigger="command",
            trigger_key=command_key(command.forge, command.comment_id),
            actor=command.author,
        )
        return await self._admit(trigger, registered, settings, command=command)

    async def _enabled(self, repo: str, *, number: int) -> tuple[str, ReviewSettings] | DispatchResult:
        """Gate 1."""
        registered = await self._repos.registered_as(repo)
        if registered is None:
            return self._refuse("repo_not_registered", repo=repo, number=number)
        settings = review_settings(await self._prefs.get(registered))
        if not settings.enabled:
            return self._refuse("review_disabled", repo=registered, number=number)
        return registered, settings

    async def _admit(
        self, trigger: ReviewTrigger, repo: str, settings: ReviewSettings, *, command: ReviewCommand | None
    ) -> DispatchResult:
        pr = trigger.pr
        ids = {"repo": repo, "number": pr.number, "trigger": trigger.trigger}
        if await self._is_own(pr):  # gate 2
            return self._refuse("own_pull_request", **ids)
        if command is not None:  # gate 3
            trusted, reason = await self._author_trusted(command)
            if not trusted:
                return self._refuse(reason, **ids, commenter=command.author)
        if await self._reviews.exists(trigger.trigger_key):  # gate 4
            return self._refuse("duplicate", **ids)
        refusal = await self._executor.dispatch_refusal(actor=trigger.actor or None)  # gate 5
        if refusal is not None:
            return self._refuse(f"budget_{refusal.limit or 'refused'}", **ids)
        # The UNIQUE index is the authority: two deliveries racing past the
        # exists() check above still claim the key only once.
        review_id = await self._reviews.claim(
            repo=repo, number=pr.number, head_sha=pr.head_sha, trigger=trigger.trigger, trigger_key=trigger.trigger_key
        )
        if review_id is None:
            return self._refuse("duplicate", **ids)
        review = build_review_config(trigger, repo=repo, settings=settings, review_id=review_id)
        try:
            job_id = await self._executor.start_pr_review_job(
                repo=repo, review=review, dispatched_by=trigger.actor or dispatch_actors.PR_REVIEW
            )
        except Exception:
            logger.warning("pr_review_job_create_failed", review_id=review_id, **ids, exc_info=True)
            await self._reviews.release(review_id)
            return self._refuse("dispatch_failed", **ids)
        await self._reviews.attach_job(review_id, job_id)
        logger.info("pr_review_dispatched", job_id=job_id, review_id=review_id, depth=review["depth"], **ids)
        return DispatchResult(dispatched=True, reason="ok", job_id=job_id)

    async def _is_own(self, pr: PullRequestInfo) -> bool:
        if is_embry0_branch(pr.head_ref):
            return True
        for job in await self._jobs.find_by_pr_url(pr.url):
            if job.get("pipeline_template") not in _NOT_AUTHORING_PIPELINES:
                return True
        return False

    async def _author_trusted(self, command: ReviewCommand) -> tuple[bool, str]:
        if command.forge == "github":
            roles = parse_author_roles(getattr(self._config, "comment_dispatch_author_roles", None) or DEFAULT_AUTHOR_ROLES)
            return check_comment_author(dict(command.github_comment or {}), roles)
        if self._gitlab is None:
            return False, "gitlab_not_configured"
        if command.gitlab_user_id is None:
            return False, "author_unknown"
        try:
            level = await self._gitlab.get_member_access_level(command.repo, command.gitlab_user_id)
        except Exception:
            logger.warning("pr_review_member_lookup_failed", repo=command.repo, exc_info=True)
            level = None
        if level is None or level < GITLAB_DEVELOPER_ACCESS:
            return False, f"access_level_{level}_below_developer"
        return True, "ok"

    async def _fetch_pr(self, forge: str, repo: str, number: int) -> PullRequestInfo | None:
        try:
            if forge == "gitlab":
                if self._gitlab is None:
                    return None
                mr = await self._gitlab.get_merge_request(repo, number)
                return pr_from_gitlab(mr, repo=repo) if mr else None
            if self._github is None:
                return None
            pull = await self._github.get_pull(repo, number)
            return pr_from_github(pull, repo=repo) if pull else None
        except Exception:
            logger.warning("pr_review_pr_fetch_failed", forge=forge, repo=repo, number=number, exc_info=True)
            return None

    def _refuse(self, reason: str, **ids: Any) -> DispatchResult:
        logger.info("pr_review_refused", reason=reason, **ids)
        return DispatchResult(dispatched=False, reason=reason)
```

```bash
git commit -F - <<'MSG'
feat(pr-review): dispatch pull request reviews through five gates

Every review trigger now passes the same five gates before a job is
created: the repo is registered with review enabled, the pull request
is not embry0's own, a commenter has write access (GitLab: Developer
or higher through the members API), the trigger key is new, and the
budget gate allows the actor.

A refused trigger holds no key, and a job that cannot be created gives
its key back, so a redelivery can still run it. Forks are always
reviewed read-only.

Refs: #30
Claude-Session: https://claude.ai/code/session_01Q5pSvJSg6T12CagYZMAHYy
MSG
```

**Interfaces:**

- Produces: GitHub `pull_request` responses keep their merge-sync shape and gain `"pr_review": {...}` only when the dispatcher answered; `issue_comment` with `/embry0 review` on a PR returns `{"status", "action": "pr_review", "dispatched", "reason", "job_id"?}` and never reaches comment dispatch or `/answer`; GitLab `merge_request` and `note` deliveries go to the dispatcher, everything else to `GitLabSyncService` as before.

- [ ] **Step 1: Write the failing webhook tests**

Create `tests/unit/api/v1/test_webhooks_pr_review.py`:

```python
"""Webhook routes hand review triggers to the dispatcher (#30)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from contextlib import asynccontextmanager
from typing import Any

import pytest
from httpx import ASGITransport, AsyncClient

from embry0.api.app import create_app
from embry0.config import Embry0Config
from embry0.services.merge_sync.recorder import RecordResult
from embry0.services.pr_review.dispatcher import DispatchResult
from tests.fixtures.webhooks import (
    github_issue_comment,
    github_review_pull_request,
    gitlab_issue,
    gitlab_merge_request,
    gitlab_mr_note,
)


class _Dispatcher:
    def __init__(self, result: DispatchResult | None = DispatchResult(True, "ok", "job-1"), *, boom: bool = False) -> None:
        self.result = result
        self.boom = boom
        self.calls: list[str] = []

    async def _answer(self, name: str) -> DispatchResult | None:
        self.calls.append(name)
        if self.boom:
            raise RuntimeError("forge down")
        return self.result

    async def handle_github_pull_request(self, payload: Any, action: str) -> DispatchResult | None:
        return await self._answer(f"pull_request:{action}")

    async def handle_github_comment(self, payload: Any) -> DispatchResult | None:
        return await self._answer("comment")

    async def handle_gitlab_merge_request(self, payload: Any) -> DispatchResult | None:
        return await self._answer("merge_request")

    async def handle_gitlab_note(self, payload: Any) -> DispatchResult | None:
        return await self._answer("note")


class _Recorder:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.calls = 0

    async def record(self, fact: Any, **_kwargs: Any) -> RecordResult:
        self.calls += 1
        return RecordResult(pr_url=fact.url, state=fact.state.value, matched_jobs=["job-0"])


class _CommentDispatch:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.calls = 0

    async def handle_pr_comment(self, payload: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
        self.calls += 1
        return {"dispatched": True, "job_id": "job-followup"}


class _GitLabSync:
    def __init__(self) -> None:
        self.calls: list[Any] = []

    @property
    def name(self) -> str:
        return "gitlab"

    async def handle_webhook_event(self, payload: Any, ctx: Any) -> dict[str, Any]:
        self.calls.append(payload)
        return {"status": "accepted", "dispatched": True}


class _IntegrationRepo:
    async def get_trigger_labels(self) -> list[str]:
        return ["embry0"]


def _app(dispatcher: _Dispatcher | None):
    @asynccontextmanager
    async def _noop(_app):
        yield

    config = Embry0Config(_env_file=None, auth_dev_mode=True, webhook_dev_mode=True, github_webhook_secret="")
    app = create_app(config, lifespan_override=_noop)
    app.state.config = config
    app.state.delivery_recorder = _Recorder()
    app.state.comment_dispatch = _CommentDispatch()
    app.state.gitlab_sync = _GitLabSync()
    app.state.issues_repo = object()
    app.state.issue_executor = object()
    app.state.integration_repo = _IntegrationRepo()
    if dispatcher is not None:
        app.state.pr_review_dispatcher = dispatcher
    return app


async def _post(app, path: str, payload: dict[str, Any], event: str | None = None) -> dict[str, Any]:
    headers = {"Content-Type": "application/json", "X-GitHub-Delivery": "d-1"}
    if event:
        headers["X-GitHub-Event"] = event
    async with AsyncClient(transport=ASGITransport(app=app), base_url="http://test") as client:
        resp = await client.post(path, content=json.dumps(payload).encode(), headers=headers)
    assert resp.status_code == 200, resp.text
    return dict(resp.json())


async def test_ready_for_review_reaches_the_dispatcher_and_skips_merge_sync() -> None:
    dispatcher = _Dispatcher()
    app = _app(dispatcher)
    body = await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook", github_review_pull_request(), "pull_request")
    assert dispatcher.calls == ["pull_request:ready_for_review"]
    assert body["pr_review"] == {"dispatched": True, "reason": "ok", "job_id": "job-1"}
    assert app.state.delivery_recorder.calls == 0, "ready_for_review carries no delivery state to record"


async def test_opened_keeps_its_merge_sync_bookkeeping_beside_the_review() -> None:
    dispatcher = _Dispatcher()
    app = _app(dispatcher)
    body = await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook", github_review_pull_request(action="opened"), "pull_request")
    assert app.state.delivery_recorder.calls == 1, "the review must not replace merge-sync bookkeeping"
    assert body["status"] == "accepted" and body["action"] == "pr_opened"
    assert body["pr_review"]["job_id"] == "job-1"


async def test_a_dispatcher_that_says_not_a_trigger_leaves_the_response_unchanged() -> None:
    dispatcher = _Dispatcher(result=None)
    app = _app(dispatcher)
    body = await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook", github_review_pull_request(action="opened"), "pull_request")
    assert dispatcher.calls == ["pull_request:opened"], "every pull_request delivery must be offered to the reviewer"
    assert "pr_review" not in body


async def test_a_failing_dispatcher_never_fails_the_delivery() -> None:
    app = _app(_Dispatcher(boom=True))
    body = await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook", github_review_pull_request(action="opened"), "pull_request")
    assert app.state.delivery_recorder.calls == 1
    assert body["pr_review"] == {"dispatched": False, "reason": "dispatch_error"}


async def test_a_review_command_is_never_a_follow_up_or_an_answer() -> None:
    dispatcher = _Dispatcher()
    app = _app(dispatcher)
    payload = github_issue_comment(body="@embry0 have a look\n/embry0 review", on_pull_request=True)
    body = await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook", payload, "issue_comment")
    assert dispatcher.calls == ["comment"]
    assert app.state.comment_dispatch.calls == 0, "a /embry0 review comment was also dispatched as a follow-up"
    assert body == {"status": "accepted", "action": "pr_review", "dispatched": True, "reason": "ok", "job_id": "job-1"}

    # …and a comment with only the tag still reaches comment dispatch, never the reviewer.
    await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook", github_issue_comment(body="@embry0 fix it", on_pull_request=True), "issue_comment")
    assert dispatcher.calls == ["comment"], "a comment without the command reached the reviewer"
    assert app.state.comment_dispatch.calls == 1


async def test_a_review_command_without_a_dispatcher_is_still_not_a_follow_up() -> None:
    app = _app(None)
    payload = github_issue_comment(body="@embry0 /embry0 review\n/embry0 review", on_pull_request=True)
    body = await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook", payload, "issue_comment")
    assert app.state.comment_dispatch.calls == 0
    assert body["status"] == "ignored" and body["reason"] == "pr_review_not_configured"


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("payload", "call"),
    [(gitlab_merge_request(), "merge_request"), (gitlab_mr_note(), "note")],
    ids=["merge_request", "note"],
)
async def test_gitlab_merge_request_and_note_events_reach_the_dispatcher(payload: dict, call: str) -> None:
    dispatcher = _Dispatcher()
    app = _app(dispatcher)
    body = await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook/gitlab", payload)
    assert dispatcher.calls == [call]
    assert body == {"status": "accepted", "action": "pr_review", "dispatched": True, "reason": "ok", "job_id": "job-1"}
    assert app.state.gitlab_sync.calls == [], f"the {call} event still went to the GitLab issue sync"

    # An issue event still goes to the GitLab issue sync, never the reviewer.
    await _post(app, "/api/v1/webhook/gitlab", gitlab_issue(action="update", labels=["embry0"], previous_labels=[]))
    assert dispatcher.calls == [call]
    assert len(app.state.gitlab_sync.calls) == 1
```

Add this helper right before the `# GitHub event handlers` banner comment:

```python
async def _pr_review(request: Request, method: str, *args: Any) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
    """Hand a delivery to the pull request reviewer (#30); None when it is not a review trigger.

    Never raises: a review that cannot start must not fail the delivery, or
    the merge-sync bookkeeping that rides the same event.
    """
    dispatcher = getattr(request.app.state, "pr_review_dispatcher", None)
    if dispatcher is None:
        return None
    try:
        result = await getattr(dispatcher, method)(*args)
    except Exception:
        logger.warning("pr_review_dispatch_failed", method=method, exc_info=True)
        return {"dispatched": False, "reason": "dispatch_error"}
    return None if result is None else dict(result.as_dict())


def _pr_review_response(review: dict[str, Any]) -> dict[str, Any]:
    return {"status": "accepted" if review.get("dispatched") else "ignored", "action": "pr_review", **review}
```

In `gitlab_webhook`, after the `if gitlab_sync is None:` early return and before the `logger.info("gitlab_webhook_received", ...)` call, insert:

```python

    # they reached GitLabSyncService, which ignores everything but issues.
    kind = payload.get("object_kind")
    if kind in ("merge_request", "note"):
        method = "handle_gitlab_merge_request" if kind == "merge_request" else "handle_gitlab_note"
        review = await _pr_review(request, method, payload)
        if review is None:
            return {"status": "ignored", "reason": f"not a pull request review trigger: {kind}"}
        return _pr_review_response(review)
```

Rename the existing `_handle_pull_request` to `_record_pull_request` (body unchanged, docstring first line becomes "PR ↔ job linking and delivery state. Creates no jobs — bookkeeping only."), and add above it:

```python
async def _handle_pull_request(request: Request, payload: dict[str, Any], action: str) -> dict[str, Any]:
    """Merge-sync bookkeeping, then the pull request review trigger (#30).

    The two are independent: ``opened`` is both a delivery state and a
    possible review trigger, ``ready_for_review`` is only the latter.
    """
    result = await _record_pull_request(request, payload, action)
    review = await _pr_review(request, "handle_github_pull_request", payload, action)
    if review is not None:
        result = {**result, "pr_review": review}
    return result
```

`_GITHUB_EVENT_HANDLERS["pull_request"]` keeps pointing at `_handle_pull_request`.

```python
"""Which lines of a pull request can carry an inline comment (#30). Pure."""

from __future__ import annotations

import pytest

from embry0.workflows.pr_review.diff_positions import (
    FileDiff,
    NewLine,
    build_line_index,
    commentable_lines,
    files_from_github,
    files_from_gitlab,
    normalize_finding_path,

# A real two-hunk patch as GitHub's pulls/{n}/files returns it (no file header).
PATCH = "\n".join(
    [
        "@@ -1,4 +1,5 @@",
        " import os",
        "-import sys",
        "+import json",
        "+import logging",
        " ",
        " def main():",
        "@@ -20,3 +21,4 @@ def main():",
        "     a = 1",
        "     b = 2",
        "     c = 3",
        "+    d = 4",
    ]
)


def test_added_and_context_lines_are_commentable_with_their_base_numbers() -> None:
    lines = commentable_lines(PATCH)
    assert lines == {
        1: NewLine("context", 1),
        2: NewLine("added", None),
        3: NewLine("added", None),
        4: NewLine("context", 3),
        5: NewLine("context", 4),
        21: NewLine("context", 20),
        22: NewLine("context", 21),
        23: NewLine("context", 22),
        24: NewLine("added", None),
    }


def test_deleted_lines_have_no_new_side_number() -> None:
    # `-import sys` was base line 2; no new-side line may claim it.
    assert all(not (nl.kind == "context" and nl.old_line == 2) for nl in commentable_lines(PATCH).values())


@pytest.mark.parametrize("line", [6, 20, 25, 0])
def test_a_line_just_outside_a_hunk_is_not_commentable(line: int) -> None:
    assert line not in commentable_lines(PATCH), f"line {line} is outside every hunk"


def test_a_hunk_header_without_counts_and_a_missing_newline_marker() -> None:
    patch = "@@ -1 +1 @@\n-old\n\\ No newline at end of file\n+new\n\\ No newline at end of file"
    assert commentable_lines(patch) == {1: NewLine("added", None)}


def test_a_file_header_before_the_first_hunk_is_skipped() -> None:
    patch = "diff --git a/x.py b/x.py\nindex 1..2 100644\n--- a/x.py\n+++ b/x.py\n@@ -0,0 +1,2 @@\n+a\n+b"
    assert commentable_lines(patch) == {1: NewLine("added", None), 2: NewLine("added", None)}


def test_an_empty_context_line_counts() -> None:
    # Some tools strip the single space of an empty context line.
    patch = "@@ -1,3 +1,3 @@\n a\n\n-b\n+c"
    assert commentable_lines(patch) == {1: NewLine("context", 1), 2: NewLine("context", 2), 3: NewLine("added", None)}


def test_binary_and_patchless_files_are_not_commentable() -> None:
    github = files_from_github(
        [
            {"filename": "logo.png", "status": "modified"},  # GitHub omits patch for binary and huge files
            {"filename": "big.json", "status": "modified", "patch": None},
        ]
    )
    gitlab = files_from_gitlab(
        [
            {"old_path": "logo.png", "new_path": "logo.png", "diff": ""},
            {"old_path": "icon.png", "new_path": "icon.png", "diff": "Binary files a/icon.png and b/icon.png differ\n"},
        ]
    )
    index = build_line_index([*github, *gitlab])
    assert {path: dict(entry.lines) for path, entry in index.items()} == {
        "logo.png": {},
        "big.json": {},
        "icon.png": {},
    }


def test_renamed_file_is_keyed_by_new_path() -> None:
    github = files_from_github(
        [{"filename": "src/new_name.py", "previous_filename": "src/old_name.py", "status": "renamed", "patch": "@@ -3,2 +3,3 @@\n x = 1\n+y = 2\n z = 3"}]
    )
    gitlab = files_from_gitlab(
        [
            {
                "old_path": "lib/old.rb",
                "new_path": "lib/new.rb",
                "renamed_file": True,
                "diff": "@@ -1,1 +1,2 @@\n a\n+b\n",
            }
        ]
    )
    index = build_line_index([*github, *gitlab])
    assert index["src/new_name.py"].old_path == "src/old_name.py"
    assert dict(index["src/new_name.py"].lines) == {3: NewLine("context", 3), 4: NewLine("added", None), 5: NewLine("context", 4)}
    assert index["lib/new.rb"].old_path == "lib/old.rb"
    assert "src/old_name.py" not in index and "lib/old.rb" not in index


def test_a_deleted_file_has_no_commentable_lines() -> None:
    github = files_from_github([{"filename": "gone.py", "status": "removed", "patch": "@@ -1,2 +0,0 @@\n-a\n-b"}])
    gitlab = files_from_gitlab([{"old_path": "old.rb", "new_path": "old.rb", "deleted_file": True, "diff": "@@ -1 +0,0 @@\n-a\n"}])
    index = build_line_index([*github, *gitlab])
    assert dict(index["gone.py"].lines) == {} and dict(index["old.rb"].lines) == {}


def test_malformed_entries_are_dropped() -> None:
    assert files_from_github([{"status": "modified"}, "not-a-dict"]) == []  # type: ignore[list-item]
    assert files_from_gitlab([{"diff": "@@ -1 +1 @@\n+a"}]) == []


def test_file_diff_keeps_both_paths() -> None:
    assert files_from_gitlab([{"old_path": "a.py", "new_path": "a.py", "diff": "@@ -1 +1 @@\n-a\n+b"}]) == [
        FileDiff(path="a.py", old_path="a.py", patch="@@ -1 +1 @@\n-a\n+b", deleted=False)
    ]


@pytest.mark.parametrize(
    ("raw", "expected"),
    [
        ("src/a.py", "src/a.py"),
        ("./src/a.py", "src/a.py"),
        ("/workspace/src/a.py", "src/a.py"),
        ("workspace/src/a.py", "src/a.py"),
        ("/src/a.py", "src/a.py"),
        ("  src\\a.py ", "src/a.py"),
        ("", ""),
    ],
)
def test_paths_are_normalised(raw: str, expected: str) -> None:
    assert normalize_finding_path(raw) == expected
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Implement the mapping**

Create `embry0/workflows/pr_review/__init__.py`:

```python
"""pr-review pipeline: review a pull request and post the findings on it (#30)."""
```

Create `embry0/workflows/pr_review/diff_positions.py`:

```python
"""Which lines of a pull request can carry an inline comment (#30).

Both forges accept an inline comment only on a line of the diff: GitHub on
any line of a hunk (``side: RIGHT`` for the new version), GitLab on an added
line by its new number, or on a context line by both numbers. A finding on
any other line is refused (GitHub answers 422 for the whole review), so the
publisher asks this module first and lists the rest in the summary.

Pure: patches in, line maps out.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import re
from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass, field
from typing import Any, Literal

_HUNK_RE = re.compile(r"^@@ -(\d+)(?:,\d+)? \+(\d+)(?:,\d+)? @@")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class NewLine:
    kind: Literal["added", "context"]
    old_line: int | None  # the base-side number of a context line; None for an added line


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FileDiff:
    path: str
    old_path: str
    patch: str | None
    deleted: bool = False


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class FileIndex:
    old_path: str
    lines: Mapping[int, NewLine] = field(default_factory=dict)


def commentable_lines(patch: str | None) -> dict[int, NewLine]:
    """New-side line number → what that line is, for every line inside a hunk."""
    lines: dict[int, NewLine] = {}
    if not patch:
        return lines
    old = new = 0
    in_hunk = False
    for raw in patch.splitlines():
        header = _HUNK_RE.match(raw)
        if header:
            old, new = int(header.group(1)), int(header.group(2))
            in_hunk = True
            continue
        if not in_hunk or raw.startswith("\\"):
            continue  # file headers, "Binary files … differ", "\ No newline at end of file"
        tag = raw[:1]
        if tag == "+":
            lines[new] = NewLine("added", None)
            new += 1
        elif tag == "-":
            old += 1
        elif tag in (" ", ""):
            lines[new] = NewLine("context", old)
            old += 1
            new += 1
        else:
            in_hunk = False  # the next file's "diff --git" in a multi-file patch
    return lines


def _str(value: Any) -> str:
    return value if isinstance(value, str) else ""


def files_from_github(items: Iterable[Mapping[str, Any]]) -> list[FileDiff]:
    files: list[FileDiff] = []
    for item in items:
        if not isinstance(item, Mapping):
            continue
        path = _str(item.get("filename"))
        if not path:
            continue
        patch = item.get("patch")
        files.append(
            FileDiff(
                path=path,
                old_path=_str(item.get("previous_filename")) or path,
                patch=patch if isinstance(patch, str) and patch else None,
                deleted=item.get("status") == "removed",
            )
        )
    return files


def files_from_gitlab(items: Iterable[Mapping[str, Any]]) -> list[FileDiff]:
    files: list[FileDiff] = []
    for item in items:
        if not isinstance(item, Mapping):
            continue
        path = _str(item.get("new_path"))
        if not path:
            continue
        diff = item.get("diff")
        files.append(
            FileDiff(
                path=path,
                old_path=_str(item.get("old_path")) or path,
                patch=diff if isinstance(diff, str) and diff else None,
                deleted=item.get("deleted_file") is True,
            )
        )
    return files


def build_line_index(files: Iterable[FileDiff]) -> dict[str, FileIndex]:
    """New path → its commentable lines. A deleted file is listed with none."""
    return {
        f.path: FileIndex(old_path=f.old_path, lines={} if f.deleted else commentable_lines(f.patch)) for f in files
    }


def normalize_finding_path(path: str) -> str:
    """A path as the agent wrote it → repository-relative, forward slashes."""
    normalized = path.strip().replace("\\", "/")
    for prefix in ("/workspace/", "workspace/"):
        if normalized.startswith(prefix):
            normalized = normalized[len(prefix) :]
    while normalized.startswith("./"):
        normalized = normalized[2:]
    return normalized.lstrip("/")
```

```bash
Both forges refuse an inline comment on a line outside the diff, and
GitHub refuses the whole review for one bad line. This maps each
file's patch to the new-side lines a comment may land on, with the
base-side number GitLab needs for a context line.

Binary, patch-less and deleted files have no commentable lines, and a
renamed file is keyed by its new path.
```

```python
"""Reading the pull request reviewer's verdict (#30)."""

from __future__ import annotations

import json

import pytest

from embry0.workflows.pr_review.verdict import Finding, Verdict, parse_verdict

VERDICT = {
    "summary": "Solid change; one real bug.",
    "review_comments": [
        {"file": "src/app.py", "line": 12, "severity": "critical", "comment": "Off by one."},
        {"file": "README.md", "line": 3, "severity": "suggestion", "comment": "Typo."},
    ],
    "checks_run": [{"name": "unit", "command": "pytest -q", "status": "failed", "output_tail": "1 failed"}],
    "suggested_dev_checks": [{"name": "unit", "paths": ["src"], "command": "pytest -q"}],
    "previous_findings": [{"file": "src/app.py", "line": 3, "comment": "Unused import.", "status": "resolved"}],
}


def test_bare_json_is_read() -> None:
    verdict = parse_verdict(json.dumps(VERDICT))
    assert verdict is not None
    assert verdict.summary == "Solid change; one real bug."
    assert verdict.findings[0] == Finding(file="src/app.py", line=12, severity="critical", comment="Off by one.")
    assert verdict.checks_run[0].status == "failed" and verdict.suggested_dev_checks[0].paths == ("src",)
    assert verdict.previous_findings[0].status == "resolved"


def test_a_fenced_block_after_prose_is_read() -> None:
    text = "I ran the tests.\n\n```json\n" + json.dumps(VERDICT) + "\n```"
    assert parse_verdict(text) is not None


def test_a_bare_object_after_prose_is_read_even_without_a_decision_key() -> None:

    ],
)
def test_an_unreadable_verdict_is_none(text) -> None:

            {"file": "a.py", "line": 2, "severity": "blocker", "comment": "unknown severity"},
        ],
        "checks_run": [{"name": "x", "command": "y", "status": "weird"}, {}],
        "previous_findings": [{"file": "a.py", "comment": "c", "status": "maybe"}],
    }
    verdict = parse_verdict(json.dumps(raw))
    assert verdict is not None
    assert [(f.severity, f.comment) for f in verdict.findings] == [("critical", "upper"), ("suggestion", "unknown severity")]
    assert [c.status for c in verdict.checks_run] == ["error"]
    assert verdict.previous_findings == ()


def test_a_verdict_round_trips_through_its_dict() -> None:
    verdict = parse_verdict(json.dumps(VERDICT))
    assert verdict is not None
    assert Verdict.from_dict(verdict.to_dict()) == verdict
    assert Verdict.from_dict(None) is None and Verdict.from_dict({"summary": ""}) is None
```

```python
"""Which findings go inline, and which into the summary (#30)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from embry0.workflows.pr_review.diff_positions import FileIndex, NewLine
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.placement import INLINE_CAP, by_severity, place_findings
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.verdict import Finding

INDEX = {
    "src/app.py": FileIndex(
        old_path="src/app.py",
        lines={10: NewLine("added", None), 11: NewLine("context", 9), 12: NewLine("added", None)},
    ),
    "src/new.py": FileIndex(old_path="src/old.py", lines={1: NewLine("added", None)}),
}


def _f(file: str, line: int | None, severity: str = "important", comment: str = "c") -> Finding:
    return Finding(file=file, line=line, severity=severity, comment=comment)


def test_a_finding_on_a_diff_line_goes_inline_with_its_positions() -> None:
    placement = place_findings([_f("src/app.py", 11)], INDEX)
    assert len(placement.inline) == 1
    comment = placement.inline[0]
    assert (comment.path, comment.old_path, comment.line, comment.old_line, comment.kind) == (
        "src/app.py",
        "src/app.py",
        11,
        9,
        "context",
    )
    assert placement.listed == ()


def test_a_line_just_outside_a_hunk_goes_elsewhere() -> None:
    placement = place_findings([_f("src/app.py", 13), _f("src/app.py", 9)], INDEX)
    assert placement.inline == () and len(placement.elsewhere) == 2


def test_files_outside_the_diff_and_findings_without_a_line_go_elsewhere() -> None:
    placement = place_findings([_f("docs/guide.md", 4), _f("src/app.py", None), _f("", None)], INDEX)
    assert placement.inline == () and len(placement.elsewhere) == 3


def test_paths_are_normalised_before_placement() -> None:
    findings = [_f("./src/app.py", 10), _f("/workspace/src/app.py", 12), _f("b/src/new.py", 1)]
    placement = place_findings(findings, INDEX)
    assert [(c.path, c.line) for c in placement.inline] == [("src/app.py", 10), ("src/app.py", 12), ("src/new.py", 1)]
    assert placement.inline[2].old_path == "src/old.py", "a renamed file keeps its old path for GitLab"


def test_the_cap_keeps_the_worst_findings_inline() -> None:
    index = {"a.py": FileIndex(old_path="a.py", lines={n: NewLine("added", None) for n in range(1, 41)})}
    findings = [_f("a.py", n, "suggestion") for n in range(1, 31)] + [_f("a.py", n, "critical") for n in range(31, 36)]
    placement = place_findings(findings, index)
    assert len(placement.inline) == INLINE_CAP == 30
    assert [c.finding.severity for c in placement.inline[:5]] == ["critical"] * 5, "critical findings must go inline first"
    assert len(placement.overflow) == 5 and {f.severity for f in placement.overflow} == {"suggestion"}
    assert placement.listed == placement.elsewhere + placement.overflow


def test_severity_order_is_stable() -> None:
    ordered = by_severity([_f("a", 1, "suggestion", "s1"), _f("a", 2, "critical", "c1"), _f("a", 3, "suggestion", "s2")])
    assert [f.comment for f in ordered] == ["c1", "s1", "s2"]
```

```python
"""The review's summary body (#30)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from embry0.services.pr_review.triggers import has_review_command
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.summary import (
    BODY_LIMIT,
    SummaryContext,
    inline_body,
    render_summary,
    review_marker,
)
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.verdict import CheckRun, Finding, PreviousFinding, SuggestedCheck, Verdict

SHA = "a" * 40
CTX = SummaryContext(job_id="job-1", head_sha=SHA, job_url="https://embry0.example.com/jobs/job-1", from_fork=False, depth="full")


def _verdict(**overrides) -> Verdict:
    base = dict(
        summary="Careful change. One bug in the retry loop.",
        findings=(
            Finding("src/app.py", 12, "critical", "Off by one."),
            Finding("src/app.py", 30, "important", "Unhandled error."),
            Finding("README.md", None, "suggestion", "Typo."),
        ),
        checks_run=(
            CheckRun("unit", "pytest -q", "passed", ""),
            CheckRun("lint", "ruff check .", "failed", "E501 line too long"),
        ),
        suggested_dev_checks=(),
        previous_findings=(),
    )
    base.update(overrides)
    return Verdict(**base)


def test_the_summary_has_the_assessment_counts_checks_and_footer() -> None:
    body = render_summary(_verdict(), inline_count=2, listed=[Finding("README.md", None, "suggestion", "Typo.")], ctx=CTX)
    assert "Careful change. One bug in the retry loop." in body
    assert "1 critical · 1 important · 1 suggestion" in body
    assert "**passed** `unit`: `pytest -q`" in body
    assert "**failed** `lint`: `ruff check .`" in body and "E501 line too long" in body
    assert "### Elsewhere in the code" in body and "`README.md` — Typo." in body
    assert f"Reviewed `{SHA[:12]}`" in body and "https://embry0.example.com/jobs/job-1" in body
    assert body.rstrip().endswith(review_marker("job-1", SHA))
    assert review_marker("job-1", SHA) == f"<!-- embry0-review job=job-1 sha={SHA} -->"


def test_a_fork_says_its_checks_were_skipped() -> None:
    ctx = SummaryContext(job_id="job-1", head_sha=SHA, job_url="u", from_fork=True, depth="read_only")
    body = render_summary(_verdict(checks_run=()), inline_count=0, listed=[], ctx=ctx)
    assert "Checks skipped: this pull request comes from a fork." in body


def test_read_only_depth_says_why_nothing_ran() -> None:
    ctx = SummaryContext(job_id="job-1", head_sha=SHA, job_url="u", from_fork=False, depth="read_only")
    assert "review depth is read-only" in render_summary(_verdict(checks_run=()), inline_count=0, listed=[], ctx=ctx)


def test_a_re_review_lists_the_earlier_findings() -> None:
    verdict = _verdict(
        previous_findings=(
            PreviousFinding("src/app.py", 3, "Unused import.", "resolved"),
            PreviousFinding("src/app.py", 9, "Missing test.", "still_open"),
        )
    )
    body = render_summary(verdict, inline_count=0, listed=[], ctx=CTX)
    assert "### Earlier findings" in body
    assert "resolved — `src/app.py:3` Unused import." in body
    assert "still open — `src/app.py:9` Missing test." in body


def test_discovered_checks_are_offered_as_a_paste_ready_block() -> None:
    verdict = _verdict(suggested_dev_checks=(SuggestedCheck("unit", ("src",), "pytest -q"),))
    body = render_summary(verdict, inline_count=0, listed=[], ctx=CTX)
    assert "### Suggested `dev_checks`" in body
    assert '"command": "pytest -q"' in body and '"paths": [\n      "src"\n    ]' in body


def test_no_findings_reads_as_such() -> None:
    body = render_summary(_verdict(findings=()), inline_count=0, listed=[], ctx=CTX)
    assert "No findings." in body


def test_the_summary_never_reads_as_a_command() -> None:
    body = render_summary(_verdict(), inline_count=0, listed=[], ctx=CTX)
    assert "/embry0 review" in body, "the footer must tell people how to ask again"
    assert has_review_command(body) is False, "embry0's own summary would ask for another review"
    # Not only because of the marker: the footer's own wording must not read as the command.
    assert has_review_command(body.replace(review_marker("job-1", SHA), "")) is False, "the footer starts a line with the command"


def test_secrets_in_agent_text_are_redacted() -> None:
    token = "ghp_" + "A" * 36
    verdict = _verdict(summary=f"Found {token} in the logs.", checks_run=(CheckRun("x", "y", "failed", f"token={token}"),))
    body = render_summary(verdict, inline_count=0, listed=[], ctx=CTX)
    assert token not in body


def test_a_huge_body_is_cut_to_the_limit_and_keeps_its_footer() -> None:
    listed = [Finding(f"f{n}.py", n, "suggestion", "x" * 900) for n in range(200)]
    body = render_summary(_verdict(), inline_count=0, listed=listed, ctx=CTX)
    assert len(body) <= BODY_LIMIT
    assert body.rstrip().endswith(review_marker("job-1", SHA))


def test_an_inline_comment_starts_with_its_severity_and_carries_the_marker() -> None:
    body = inline_body(Finding("a.py", 1, "important", "Unhandled error."), marker=review_marker("job-1", SHA))
    assert body.startswith("**Important** — Unhandled error.")
    assert body.endswith(review_marker("job-1", SHA))
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Implement the verdict parser**

Create `embry0/workflows/pr_review/verdict.py`:

```python
"""The pull request reviewer's verdict: its shape and how it is read (#30).

The agent's final message is a JSON object (see ``prompts.VERDICT_CONTRACT``).
It is read with the existing reviewer parser, ``_review_payload`` (whole text,
then a fenced block). That parser's last fallback looks for an object with a
``decision`` key, which this verdict does not have, so the last object with a
``summary`` is tried after it. Anything else is no verdict: the job fails and
nothing is posted.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import Any

from embry0.orchestration.agent_json import last_object_with
from embry0.orchestration.routing.conditions import _review_payload

SEVERITIES: tuple[str, ...] = ("critical", "important", "suggestion")
CHECK_STATUSES: tuple[str, ...] = ("passed", "failed", "error")
PREVIOUS_STATUSES: tuple[str, ...] = ("resolved", "still_open")


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Finding:
    file: str
    line: int | None
    severity: str
    comment: str


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class CheckRun:
    name: str
    command: str
    status: str
    output_tail: str


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SuggestedCheck:
    name: str
    paths: tuple[str, ...]
    command: str


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PreviousFinding:
    file: str
    line: int | None
    comment: str
    status: str


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Verdict:
    summary: str
    findings: tuple[Finding, ...]
    checks_run: tuple[CheckRun, ...]
    suggested_dev_checks: tuple[SuggestedCheck, ...]
    previous_findings: tuple[PreviousFinding, ...]

    def to_dict(self) -> dict[str, Any]:
        """The agent's JSON shape, which is also what ``pr_reviews.verdict`` stores."""
        return {
            "summary": self.summary,
            "review_comments": [
                {"file": f.file, "line": f.line, "severity": f.severity, "comment": f.comment} for f in self.findings
            ],
            "checks_run": [
                {"name": c.name, "command": c.command, "status": c.status, "output_tail": c.output_tail}
                for c in self.checks_run
            ],
            "suggested_dev_checks": [
                {"name": s.name, "paths": list(s.paths), "command": s.command} for s in self.suggested_dev_checks
            ],
            "previous_findings": [
                {"file": p.file, "line": p.line, "comment": p.comment, "status": p.status}
                for p in self.previous_findings
            ],
        }

    @classmethod
    def from_dict(cls, data: Any) -> Verdict | None:
        return verdict_from_mapping(data) if isinstance(data, Mapping) else None


def _text(value: Any) -> str:
    return value.strip() if isinstance(value, str) else ""


def _line(value: Any) -> int | None:
    if isinstance(value, bool):
        return None
    if isinstance(value, int):
        return value if value > 0 else None
    if isinstance(value, str) and value.strip().isdigit():
        number = int(value.strip())
        return number if number > 0 else None
    return None


def _list(data: Mapping[str, Any], key: str) -> list[Any]:
    value = data.get(key)
    return value if isinstance(value, list) else []


def _finding(raw: Any) -> Finding | None:
    if not isinstance(raw, Mapping):
        return None
    comment = _text(raw.get("comment"))
    if not comment:
        return None
    severity = _text(raw.get("severity")).lower()
    return Finding(
        file=_text(raw.get("file")),
        line=_line(raw.get("line")),
        severity=severity if severity in SEVERITIES else "suggestion",
        comment=comment,
    )


def _check(raw: Any) -> CheckRun | None:
    if not isinstance(raw, Mapping):
        return None
    name, command = _text(raw.get("name")), _text(raw.get("command"))
    if not (name or command):
        return None
    status = _text(raw.get("status")).lower()
    return CheckRun(
        name=name,
        command=command,
        status=status if status in CHECK_STATUSES else "error",
        output_tail=_text(raw.get("output_tail")),
    )


def _suggested(raw: Any) -> SuggestedCheck | None:
    if not isinstance(raw, Mapping):
        return None
    command = _text(raw.get("command"))
    if not command:
        return None
    paths = tuple(p.strip().strip("/") for p in _list(raw, "paths") if isinstance(p, str) and p.strip().strip("/"))
    return SuggestedCheck(name=_text(raw.get("name")) or "check", paths=paths, command=command)


def _previous(raw: Any) -> PreviousFinding | None:
    if not isinstance(raw, Mapping):
        return None
    status = _text(raw.get("status")).lower()
    comment = _text(raw.get("comment"))
    if status not in PREVIOUS_STATUSES or not comment:
        return None
    return PreviousFinding(file=_text(raw.get("file")), line=_line(raw.get("line")), comment=comment, status=status)


def verdict_from_mapping(data: Mapping[str, Any]) -> Verdict | None:
    summary = _text(data.get("summary"))
    if not summary:
        return None
    if "review_comments" in data and not isinstance(data["review_comments"], list):
        return None
    return Verdict(
        summary=summary,
        findings=tuple(f for f in (_finding(r) for r in _list(data, "review_comments")) if f is not None),
        checks_run=tuple(c for c in (_check(r) for r in _list(data, "checks_run")) if c is not None),
        suggested_dev_checks=tuple(
            s for s in (_suggested(r) for r in _list(data, "suggested_dev_checks")) if s is not None
        ),
        previous_findings=tuple(p for p in (_previous(r) for r in _list(data, "previous_findings")) if p is not None),
    )


def parse_verdict(text: Any) -> Verdict | None:
    raw = text if isinstance(text, str) else ""
    data = _review_payload(raw)
    verdict = verdict_from_mapping(data) if data is not None else None
    if verdict is not None:
        return verdict
    fallback = last_object_with(raw, "summary")
    return verdict_from_mapping(fallback) if fallback is not None else None
```

- [ ] **Step 6: Implement placement**

Create `embry0/workflows/pr_review/placement.py`:

```python
"""Which findings become inline comments (#30).

A finding goes inline only when its file and line are a commentable line of
the diff (``diff_positions``). Inline comments are capped at
:data:`INLINE_CAP`, worst severity first; everything else is listed in the
summary under "Elsewhere in the code".
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from collections.abc import Iterable, Mapping
from dataclasses import dataclass

from embry0.workflows.pr_review.diff_positions import FileIndex, normalize_finding_path
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.verdict import Finding

INLINE_CAP = 30
_RANK = {"critical": 0, "important": 1, "suggestion": 2}


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class InlineComment:
    finding: Finding
    path: str
    old_path: str
    line: int
    old_line: int | None
    kind: str  # "added" | "context"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class Placement:
    inline: tuple[InlineComment, ...]
    elsewhere: tuple[Finding, ...]  # not on a commentable line
    overflow: tuple[Finding, ...]  # commentable, but past the cap

    @property
    def listed(self) -> tuple[Finding, ...]:
        return self.elsewhere + self.overflow


def by_severity(findings: Iterable[Finding]) -> list[Finding]:
    """Worst first; the agent's order within a severity."""
    return sorted(findings, key=lambda f: _RANK.get(f.severity, len(_RANK)))


def _locate(finding: Finding, index: Mapping[str, FileIndex]) -> InlineComment | None:
    if finding.line is None:
        return None
    path = normalize_finding_path(finding.file)
    entry = index.get(path)
    if entry is None and path[:2] in ("a/", "b/"):  # a path copied from a git diff header
        path = path[2:]
        entry = index.get(path)
    if entry is None:
        return None
    new_line = entry.lines.get(finding.line)
    if new_line is None:
        return None
    return InlineComment(
        finding=finding,
        path=path,
        old_path=entry.old_path,
        line=finding.line,
        old_line=new_line.old_line,
        kind=new_line.kind,
    )


def place_findings(
    findings: Iterable[Finding], index: Mapping[str, FileIndex], *, cap: int = INLINE_CAP
) -> Placement:
    inline: list[InlineComment] = []
    elsewhere: list[Finding] = []
    overflow: list[Finding] = []
    for finding in by_severity(findings):
        spot = _locate(finding, index)
        if spot is None:
            elsewhere.append(finding)
        elif len(inline) < cap:
            inline.append(spot)
        else:
            overflow.append(finding)
    return Placement(inline=tuple(inline), elsewhere=tuple(elsewhere), overflow=tuple(overflow))
```

- [ ] **Step 7: Implement the summary**

Create `embry0/workflows/pr_review/summary.py`:

```python
"""The review's summary body and inline comment bodies (#30).

Everything the agent wrote is redacted before it is posted: the sandbox may
print a credential into a check's output, and the pull request's page may be
public. The footer names the reviewed sha, links the job, says how to ask for
another review — mid-line, so the body never reads as the command itself —
and ends with the hidden marker that keeps embry0 from triggering on its own
comments.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

import json
from collections import Counter
from collections.abc import Sequence
from dataclasses import dataclass

from embry0.safety.redaction import redact_bounded
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.verdict import Finding, Verdict

BODY_LIMIT = 60000  # GitHub caps a review body at 65536 characters
SUMMARY_CHARS = 8000
COMMENT_CHARS = 4000
OUTPUT_TAIL_CHARS = 1500
_LABEL = {"critical": "Critical", "important": "Important", "suggestion": "Suggestion"}
_TRUNCATED = "\n\n… (cut to fit the forge's size limit; the full verdict is on the job)"


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class SummaryContext:
    job_id: str
    head_sha: str
    job_url: str
    from_fork: bool
    depth: str


def review_marker(job_id: str, head_sha: str) -> str:
    return f"<!-- embry0-review job={job_id} sha={head_sha} -->"


def _code(text: str) -> str:
    return "`" + text.replace("`", "'") + "`"


def _where(file: str, line: int | None) -> str:
    if not file:
        return "(general)"
    return f"{file}:{line}" if line else file


def inline_body(finding: Finding, *, marker: str) -> str:
    label = _LABEL.get(finding.severity, "Suggestion")
    return f"**{label}** — {redact_bounded(finding.comment, COMMENT_CHARS)}\n\n{marker}"


def _counts(verdict: Verdict, inline_count: int, listed: int) -> str:
    if not verdict.findings:
        return "No findings."
    counts = Counter(f.severity for f in verdict.findings)
    return (
        f"**Findings:** {counts['critical']} critical · {counts['important']} important · "
        f"{counts['suggestion']} suggestion — {inline_count} inline, {listed} listed below."
    )


def _checks(verdict: Verdict, ctx: SummaryContext) -> str:
    if ctx.from_fork:
        return "### Checks\nChecks skipped: this pull request comes from a fork."
    if ctx.depth == "read_only":
        return "### Checks\nChecks skipped: this repository's review depth is read-only."
    if not verdict.checks_run:
        return "### Checks\nNo checks were run."
    lines = ["### Checks"]
    for check in verdict.checks_run:
        label = check.name or check.command
        entry = f"- **{check.status}** {_code(label)}"
        if check.command and check.command != label:
            entry += f": {_code(check.command)}"
        lines.append(entry)
        if check.status != "passed" and check.output_tail:
            tail = redact_bounded(check.output_tail, OUTPUT_TAIL_CHARS).replace("~~~", "~ ~ ~")
            lines.append("\n  ~~~\n" + "\n".join(f"  {t}" for t in tail.splitlines()) + "\n  ~~~")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _previous(verdict: Verdict) -> str:
    lines = ["### Earlier findings"]
    for p in verdict.previous_findings:
        state = "resolved" if p.status == "resolved" else "still open"
        lines.append(f"- {state} — {_code(_where(p.file, p.line))} {redact_bounded(p.comment, COMMENT_CHARS)}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _elsewhere(listed: Sequence[Finding]) -> str:
    lines = ["### Elsewhere in the code"]
    for f in listed:
        label = _LABEL.get(f.severity, "Suggestion")
        lines.append(f"- **{label}** {_code(_where(f.file, f.line))} — {redact_bounded(f.comment, COMMENT_CHARS)}")
    return "\n".join(lines)


def _suggested(verdict: Verdict) -> str:
    block = json.dumps(
        [{"name": s.name, "paths": list(s.paths), "command": s.command} for s in verdict.suggested_dev_checks],
        indent=2,
    )
    return (
        "### Suggested `dev_checks`\n"
        "This repository configures no dev checks, so the reviewer worked out these commands. "
        "To make them the repository's checks, add them under Dev checks in its embry0 preferences:\n\n"
        f"~~~json\n{block}\n~~~"
    )


def _footer(ctx: SummaryContext) -> str:
    return (
        f"---\nReviewed {_code(ctx.head_sha[:12])} · [job {ctx.job_id}]({ctx.job_url}) · "
        "For another review, comment with a line that reads `/embry0 review`.\n"
        f"{review_marker(ctx.job_id, ctx.head_sha)}"
    )


def render_summary(verdict: Verdict, *, inline_count: int, listed: Sequence[Finding], ctx: SummaryContext) -> str:
    parts = [
        "## embry0 review",
        redact_bounded(verdict.summary, SUMMARY_CHARS),
        _counts(verdict, inline_count, len(listed)),
        _checks(verdict, ctx),
    ]
    if verdict.previous_findings:
        parts.append(_previous(verdict))
    if listed:
        parts.append(_elsewhere(listed))
    if verdict.suggested_dev_checks:
        parts.append(_suggested(verdict))
    footer = _footer(ctx)
    body = "\n\n".join(parts)
    room = BODY_LIMIT - len(footer) - 2
    if len(body) > room:
        body = body[: room - len(_TRUNCATED)] + _TRUNCATED
    return f"{body}\n\n{footer}"
```

- [ ] **Step 3: Add `ForgeWrite`**

In `embry0/execution/forge_client.py`, add `from dataclasses import dataclass` to the imports and, after `retry_after_seconds`, add:

```python
@dataclass(frozen=True)
class ForgeWrite:
    """What a forge answered to one write: its status, and its own words when it refused."""

    status_code: int
    detail: str = ""

    @property
    def ok(self) -> bool:
        return 200 <= self.status_code < 300


def forge_write(response: httpx.Response) -> ForgeWrite:
    """``response`` as a :class:`ForgeWrite`; ``detail`` is the forge's message, at most 1000 characters."""
    if 200 <= response.status_code < 300:
        return ForgeWrite(status_code=response.status_code)
    detail = ""
    try:
        body: Any = response.json()
    except ValueError:
        body = None
    if isinstance(body, dict):
        message = body.get("message") or body.get("error") or ""
        errors = body.get("errors")
        detail = f"{message} {errors}".strip() if errors else str(message)
    return ForgeWrite(status_code=response.status_code, detail=(detail or response.text)[:1000])
```

- [ ] **Step 4: Add the GitHub calls**

In `embry0/execution/github_client.py`, import `ForgeWrite, forge_write` from `embry0.execution.forge_client` alongside the existing names, and add to `GitHubClient` after `list_pull_files`:

```python
    async def list_pull_file_patches(self, repo: str, number: int) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        """``pulls/{n}/files`` items with their ``patch`` (#30).

        Raises ``httpx.HTTPStatusError`` on an error page: a reviewer that
        placed comments against half the files would place them wrongly.
        GitHub omits ``patch`` for binary and very large files.
        """
        items = await self.paginate(f"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}/files", repo=repo, max_pages=30, strict=True)
        return [f for f in items if isinstance(f, dict)]

    async def create_pull_review(
        self, repo: str, number: int, *, commit_id: str | None, body: str, comments: list[dict[str, Any]]
    ) -> ForgeWrite:
        """Post one review with ``event: COMMENT`` (#30) — advisory: never APPROVE or REQUEST_CHANGES.

        ``commit_id`` pins the inline comments to the reviewed commit; None
        lets GitHub use the pull request's head (a body-only review needs no pin).
        """
        payload: dict[str, Any] = {"body": body, "event": "COMMENT", "comments": comments}
        if commit_id:
            payload = {"commit_id": commit_id, **payload}
        resp = await self.post(f"/repos/{repo}/pulls/{number}/reviews", repo=repo, json=payload)
        return forge_write(resp)
```

- [ ] **Step 5: Add the GitLab calls**

In `embry0/execution/gitlab_client.py`, add `import httpx` and `from embry0.execution.forge_client import ForgeClient, ForgeWrite, forge_write` (replacing the single-name import), then replace `create_mr_note` and add the new calls after it:

```python
    async def post_mr_note(self, repo: str, iid: int, body: str) -> ForgeWrite:
        """Post a note on a merge request; what the forge answered."""
        resp = await self.post(self.project_path(repo, f"/merge_requests/{iid}/notes"), repo=repo, json={"body": body})
        return forge_write(resp)

    async def create_mr_note(self, repo: str, iid: int, body: str) -> bool:
        """Post a note (comment) on a merge request. True when accepted."""
        return (await self.post_mr_note(repo, iid, body)).ok

    async def create_mr_discussion(self, repo: str, iid: int, *, body: str, position: dict[str, Any]) -> ForgeWrite:
        """Start a diff discussion at ``position`` (#30); GitLab answers 400 for a line outside the diff."""
        resp = await self.post(
            self.project_path(repo, f"/merge_requests/{iid}/discussions"),
            repo=repo,
            json={"body": body, "position": position},
        )
        return forge_write(resp)

    async def list_merge_request_diffs(self, repo: str, iid: int) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        """The merge request's file diffs (#30).

        ``/diffs`` exists from GitLab 15.7; an older instance answers 404 there
        and serves the same items under ``/changes``. Raises on any other error.
        """
        try:
            items = await self.paginate(
                self.project_path(repo, f"/merge_requests/{iid}/diffs"), repo=repo, strict=True
            )
        except httpx.HTTPStatusError as exc:
            if exc.response.status_code != 404:
                raise
            resp = await self.get(self.project_path(repo, f"/merge_requests/{iid}/changes"), repo=repo)
            resp.raise_for_status()
            body = resp.json()
            items = body.get("changes") or [] if isinstance(body, dict) else []
        return [item for item in items if isinstance(item, dict)]
```

`create_mr_note` used to accept only 200/201; any 2xx is now accepted, which GitLab never answers differently for this endpoint.

Create `tests/unit/workflows/pr_review/test_publish.py`:

```python
"""Posting a review on each forge, and its fallbacks (#30)."""

from __future__ import annotations

from typing import Any

import pytest

from embry0.execution.forge_client import ForgeWrite
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.publish import PublishError, publish_github, publish_gitlab
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.summary import SummaryContext, review_marker
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.verdict import Finding, Verdict

SHA = "a" * 40
CTX = SummaryContext(job_id="job-1", head_sha=SHA, job_url="https://embry0.example.com/jobs/job-1", from_fork=False, depth="full")
PATCH = "@@ -1,2 +1,3 @@\n a\n+b\n c"  # new lines: 1 context(old 1), 2 added, 3 context(old 2)
REFS = {"base_sha": "b" * 40, "start_sha": "b" * 40, "head_sha": SHA}


def _verdict(*findings: Finding) -> Verdict:
    return Verdict(summary="Reviewed.", findings=findings, checks_run=(), suggested_dev_checks=(), previous_findings=())


class _GitHub:
    def __init__(self, *, head_sha: str = SHA, patch: str = PATCH, answers: list[ForgeWrite] | None = None) -> None:
        self.head_sha = head_sha
        self.patch = patch
        self.answers = answers or [ForgeWrite(200)]
        self.reviews: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self.files_read = 0

    async def get_pull(self, repo: str, number: int) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        return {"head": {"sha": self.head_sha}}

    async def list_pull_file_patches(self, repo: str, number: int) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        self.files_read += 1
        return [{"filename": "src/app.py", "status": "modified", "patch": self.patch}]

    async def create_pull_review(self, repo: str, number: int, *, commit_id: str | None, body: str, comments: list[dict[str, Any]]) -> ForgeWrite:
        self.reviews.append({"commit_id": commit_id, "body": body, "comments": comments})
        return self.answers.pop(0)


class _GitLab:
    def __init__(self, *, diff_refs: dict[str, str] | None = REFS, refuse_lines: tuple[int, ...] = (), note: ForgeWrite = ForgeWrite(201)) -> None:
        self.diff_refs = diff_refs
        self.refuse_lines = refuse_lines
        self.note = note
        self.positions: list[dict[str, Any]] = []
        self.notes: list[str] = []

    async def get_merge_request(self, repo: str, iid: int) -> dict[str, Any] | None:
        return {"iid": iid, "diff_refs": self.diff_refs}

    async def list_merge_request_diffs(self, repo: str, iid: int) -> list[dict[str, Any]]:
        return [{"old_path": "src/app.py", "new_path": "src/app.py", "diff": PATCH}]

    async def create_mr_discussion(self, repo: str, iid: int, *, body: str, position: dict[str, Any]) -> ForgeWrite:
        self.positions.append(position)
        assert body.startswith("**") and body.endswith(review_marker("job-1", SHA))
        return ForgeWrite(400, "line_code can't be blank") if position["new_line"] in self.refuse_lines else ForgeWrite(201)

    async def post_mr_note(self, repo: str, iid: int, body: str) -> ForgeWrite:
        self.notes.append(body)
        return self.note


ON_DIFF = Finding("src/app.py", 2, "important", "Unhandled error.")
OFF_DIFF = Finding("docs/guide.md", 1, "suggestion", "Stale doc.")


# ---- GitHub ----------------------------------------------------------------


async def test_github_posts_one_comment_review_pinned_to_the_reviewed_commit() -> None:
    client = _GitHub()
    result = await publish_github(client, repo="acme/widgets", number=7, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF, OFF_DIFF), ctx=CTX)

    assert (result.inline_count, result.listed_count) == (1, 1)
    [review] = client.reviews
    assert review["commit_id"] == SHA
    assert review["comments"] == [
        {"path": "src/app.py", "line": 2, "side": "RIGHT", "body": f"**Important** — Unhandled error.\n\n{review_marker('job-1', SHA)}"}
    ]
    assert "### Elsewhere in the code" in review["body"] and "`docs/guide.md:1` — Stale doc." in review["body"]


async def test_a_422_retries_once_without_comments_or_commit_id() -> None:
    client = _GitHub(answers=[ForgeWrite(422, "Line could not be resolved"), ForgeWrite(200)])
    result = await publish_github(client, repo="acme/widgets", number=7, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF, OFF_DIFF), ctx=CTX)

    assert len(client.reviews) == 2, "a 422 must be retried exactly once"
    retry = client.reviews[1]
    assert retry["comments"] == [] and retry["commit_id"] is None
    assert "`src/app.py:2` — Unhandled error." in retry["body"] and "`docs/guide.md:1` — Stale doc." in retry["body"]
    assert (result.inline_count, result.listed_count) == (0, 2)


async def test_a_second_refusal_fails_with_the_forges_message() -> None:
    client = _GitHub(answers=[ForgeWrite(422, "first"), ForgeWrite(422, "Pull request is locked")])
    with pytest.raises(PublishError, match="422.*Pull request is locked"):
        await publish_github(client, repo="acme/widgets", number=7, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF), ctx=CTX)


async def test_a_non_422_refusal_is_not_retried() -> None:
    client = _GitHub(answers=[ForgeWrite(403, "Resource not accessible by integration")])
    with pytest.raises(PublishError, match="403"):
        await publish_github(client, repo="acme/widgets", number=7, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF), ctx=CTX)
    assert len(client.reviews) == 1


async def test_at_most_thirty_inline_comments_and_the_rest_in_the_summary() -> None:
    patch = "@@ -0,0 +1,40 @@\n" + "\n".join(f"+line {n}" for n in range(1, 41))
    findings = [Finding("src/app.py", n, "suggestion", f"note {n}") for n in range(1, 36)]
    client = _GitHub(patch=patch)
    result = await publish_github(client, repo="acme/widgets", number=7, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(*findings), ctx=CTX)

    assert len(client.reviews[0]["comments"]) == 30
    assert (result.inline_count, result.listed_count) == (30, 5)
    assert all(f"note {n}" in client.reviews[0]["body"] for n in range(31, 36))


async def test_head_moved_since_review_posts_everything_in_the_summary_on_github() -> None:
    client = _GitHub(head_sha="f" * 40)
    result = await publish_github(client, repo="acme/widgets", number=7, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF), ctx=CTX)
    assert client.files_read == 0 and client.reviews[0]["comments"] == []
    assert (result.inline_count, result.listed_count) == (0, 1)


# ---- GitLab ----------------------------------------------------------------


async def test_gitlab_posts_a_discussion_per_finding_then_one_summary_note() -> None:
    context_line = Finding("src/app.py", 3, "critical", "Wrong default.")
    client = _GitLab()
    result = await publish_gitlab(client, repo="acme/products/web", number=5, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF, context_line, OFF_DIFF), ctx=CTX)

    assert client.positions == [
        {"position_type": "text", **REFS, "old_path": "src/app.py", "new_path": "src/app.py", "new_line": 3, "old_line": 2},
        {"position_type": "text", **REFS, "old_path": "src/app.py", "new_path": "src/app.py", "new_line": 2},
    ], "context lines need both numbers, added lines only the new one; critical first"
    [note] = client.notes
    assert "`docs/guide.md:1` — Stale doc." in note and note.rstrip().endswith(review_marker("job-1", SHA))
    assert (result.inline_count, result.listed_count) == (2, 1)


async def test_a_refused_position_moves_its_finding_into_the_summary() -> None:
    client = _GitLab(refuse_lines=(2,))
    result = await publish_gitlab(client, repo="acme/products/web", number=5, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF), ctx=CTX)
    assert "`src/app.py:2` — Unhandled error." in client.notes[0]
    assert (result.inline_count, result.listed_count) == (0, 1)


async def test_a_refused_summary_fails_with_the_forges_message() -> None:
    client = _GitLab(note=ForgeWrite(500, "Internal Server Error"))
    with pytest.raises(PublishError, match="500.*Internal Server Error"):
        await publish_gitlab(client, repo="acme/products/web", number=5, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF), ctx=CTX)


@pytest.mark.parametrize("diff_refs", [None, {**REFS, "head_sha": "f" * 40}, {"base_sha": "b" * 40}], ids=["no-refs", "head-moved", "partial-refs"])
async def test_head_moved_since_review_posts_everything_in_the_summary_on_gitlab(diff_refs) -> None:
    client = _GitLab(diff_refs=diff_refs)
    result = await publish_gitlab(client, repo="acme/products/web", number=5, head_sha=SHA, verdict=_verdict(ON_DIFF), ctx=CTX)
    assert client.positions == [], "no inline comment may be placed against lines of another commit"
    assert (result.inline_count, result.listed_count) == (0, 1)
```

- [ ] **Step 10: Implement the publisher**

Create `embry0/workflows/pr_review/publish.py`:

```python
"""Post a review on the forge (#30).

Runs in the orchestrator with the forge token (``ForgeClient.token_for``);
nothing is posted from the sandbox. Inline comments are placed only when the
pull request's head is still the reviewed commit: once someone pushed, the
lines the agent named belong to another version, so every finding goes into
the summary instead.

GitHub: one ``POST pulls/{n}/reviews`` (``event: COMMENT``); on a 422 it is
retried once with every finding in the body and without ``commit_id``.
GitLab: one diff discussion per inline finding (a refused position moves the
finding into the summary), then one summary note.
"""

from __future__ import annotations

from dataclasses import dataclass
from typing import TYPE_CHECKING, Any

import structlog

from embry0.workflows.pr_review.diff_positions import FileIndex, build_line_index, files_from_github, files_from_gitlab
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.placement import by_severity, place_findings
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.summary import SummaryContext, inline_body, render_summary, review_marker
from embry0.workflows.pr_review.verdict import Finding, Verdict

if TYPE_CHECKING:
    from embry0.execution.github_client import GitHubClient
    from embry0.execution.gitlab_client import GitLabClient

logger = structlog.get_logger(__name__)

_POSITION_REFS = ("base_sha", "start_sha", "head_sha")


class PublishError(RuntimeError):
    """The forge refused the review after the fallback. The message is the forge's."""


@dataclass(frozen=True)
class PublishResult:
    inline_count: int
    listed_count: int


async def publish_github(
    client: GitHubClient, *, repo: str, number: int, head_sha: str, verdict: Verdict, ctx: SummaryContext
) -> PublishResult:
    pull = await client.get_pull(repo, number)
    head = (pull or {}).get("head")
    current = str(head.get("sha") or "") if isinstance(head, dict) else ""
    index: dict[str, FileIndex] = {}
    if current == head_sha:
        index = build_line_index(files_from_github(await client.list_pull_file_patches(repo, number)))
    else:
        logger.info("pr_review_head_moved", forge="github", repo=repo, number=number, reviewed=head_sha, current=current or None)
    placement = place_findings(verdict.findings, index)
    marker = review_marker(ctx.job_id, head_sha)
    comments = [
        {"path": c.path, "line": c.line, "side": "RIGHT", "body": inline_body(c.finding, marker=marker)}
        for c in placement.inline
    ]
    listed = list(placement.listed)
    first = await client.create_pull_review(
        repo,
        number,
        commit_id=head_sha,
        body=render_summary(verdict, inline_count=len(comments), listed=listed, ctx=ctx),
        comments=comments,
    )
    if first.ok:
        return PublishResult(inline_count=len(comments), listed_count=len(listed))
    if first.status_code != 422:
        raise PublishError(f"GitHub refused the review ({first.status_code}): {first.detail}")
    # One retry: every finding in the body and no commit pin. A line GitHub
    # could not resolve, or a head that moved, are the usual causes of a 422.
    logger.info("pr_review_github_422_fallback", repo=repo, number=number, detail=first.detail[:300])
    everything = by_severity([c.finding for c in placement.inline] + listed)
    second = await client.create_pull_review(
        repo,
        number,
        commit_id=None,
        body=render_summary(verdict, inline_count=0, listed=everything, ctx=ctx),
        comments=[],
    )
    if not second.ok:
        raise PublishError(f"GitHub refused the review ({second.status_code}): {second.detail}")
    return PublishResult(inline_count=0, listed_count=len(everything))


def _diff_refs(mr: dict[str, Any] | None) -> dict[str, str]:
    raw = (mr or {}).get("diff_refs")
    if not isinstance(raw, dict):
        return {}
    refs = {key: str(raw.get(key) or "") for key in _POSITION_REFS}
    return refs if all(refs.values()) else {}


async def publish_gitlab(
    client: GitLabClient, *, repo: str, number: int, head_sha: str, verdict: Verdict, ctx: SummaryContext
) -> PublishResult:
    refs = _diff_refs(await client.get_merge_request(repo, number))
    index: dict[str, FileIndex] = {}
    if refs and refs["head_sha"] == head_sha:
        index = build_line_index(files_from_gitlab(await client.list_merge_request_diffs(repo, number)))
    else:
        logger.info("pr_review_head_moved", forge="gitlab", repo=repo, number=number, reviewed=head_sha, current=refs.get("head_sha"))
    placement = place_findings(verdict.findings, index)
    marker = review_marker(ctx.job_id, head_sha)
    listed: list[Finding] = list(placement.listed)
    inline = 0
    for comment in placement.inline:
        position: dict[str, Any] = {
            "position_type": "text",
            **refs,
            "old_path": comment.old_path,
            "new_path": comment.path,
            "new_line": comment.line,
        }
        if comment.kind == "context" and comment.old_line is not None:
            position["old_line"] = comment.old_line
        try:
            result = await client.create_mr_discussion(
                repo, number, body=inline_body(comment.finding, marker=marker), position=position
            )
        except Exception:
            logger.warning("pr_review_gitlab_discussion_failed", repo=repo, number=number, exc_info=True)
            listed.append(comment.finding)
            continue
        if result.ok:
            inline += 1
        else:
            logger.info(
                "pr_review_gitlab_position_refused",
                repo=repo,
                number=number,
                path=comment.path,
                line=comment.line,
                status=result.status_code,
                detail=result.detail[:300],
            )
            listed.append(comment.finding)
    listed = by_severity(listed)
    note = await client.post_mr_note(repo, number, render_summary(verdict, inline_count=inline, listed=listed, ctx=ctx))
    if not note.ok:
        raise PublishError(f"GitLab refused the review summary ({note.status_code}): {note.detail}")
    return PublishResult(inline_count=inline, listed_count=len(listed))
```
